From topological amplitudes to rescattering dynamics in charmed baryon decays
Di Wang 1

Abstract
Charmed baryon decays play an important role in studying the weak and strong interactions. Charmed baryon decays decaying into an octet baryon and a pseudoscalar meson have been studied in the framework of rescattering dynamics at the hadron level and topological amplitudes at the quark level. In this work, we investigate the relation between topological amplitudes and rescattering dynamics in the B c ​ 3 ¯ → B 8 ​ P B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P decays. Note that the chiral Lagrangian is constructed using (1,1)-rank octet tensors. The (1,1)-rank amplitudes, which are linear combinations of topological diagrams, are used to match the chiral Lagrangian. Possible meson-meson or meson-baryon coupling configurations are constructed from the (1,1)-rank amplitudes via tensor contractions. The rescattering amplitudes derived from topological amplitudes are consistent with those derived directly from the chiral Lagrangian. Isospin sum rules for all isospin systems in B c ​ 3 ¯ → B 8 ​ P B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P decays are checked in terms of s s - and t t -channel rescattering amplitudes. Rescattering amplitudes contributing to quark-loop diagrams are found to be comparable to those contributing to tree diagrams, indicating potential observable C ​ P CP violation in charmed baryon decays. Furthermore, it is found that the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is not consistent with the rescattering dynamics. The proof of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is questionable when the color changes of quarks arising from gluons are considered. We suggest precisely measuring the branching fraction of the Λ c + → Σ + ​ K S 0 \Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}_{S} mode on Belle (II) to test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.



I
Introduction

Charmed baryon decays provide an important platform for studying non-perturbative baryonic transitions.
Among charmed baryon decays, the decays of the charmed baryon anti-triplet
(
B_{c\overline{3}}
) into a baryon octet (
B_{8}
) and a pseudoscalar meson (
P
) are the most widely investigated.
Extensive data on the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays have been collected by
BESIII
BESIII:2024sfz
;
BESIII:2024cbr
;
BESIII:2025vvd
;
BESIII:2022wxj
;
BESIII:2023uvs
;
BESIII:2023vfi
;
BESIII:2023ooh
;
BESIII:2023rky
;
BESIII:2023iwu
;
BESIII:2022udq
;
BESIII:2022izy
;
BESIII:2022vrr
;
BESIII:2022xne
;
BESIII:2022onh
;
BESIII:2022aok
;
BESIII:2022bkj
;
BESIII:2021fqx
;
BESIII:2020kap
;
BESIII:2020cpu
;
BESIII:2019odb
;
BESIII:2018qyg
;
Ablikim:2018jfs
;
Ablikim:2018woi
;
Ablikim:2018bir
;
Ablikim:2015prg
;
Ablikim:2015flg
;
Ablikim:2016tze
;
Ablikim:2016mcr
;
Ablikim:2016vqd
;
Ablikim:2017ors
;
Ablikim:2017iqd
,
Belle (II)
Belle:2024xcs
;
Belle-II:2025klu
;
Belle:2021dgc
;
Belle:2022uod
;
Belle:2022bsi
;
Belle:2021btl
;
Belle:2021crz
;
Pal:2017ypp
;
Berger:2018pli
;
Belle:2024ikp
;
Zupanc:2013iki
;
Yang:2015ytm

and LHCb
Aaij:2017xva
;
Aaij:2017nsd
over recent decades.
From a theoretical perspective, QCD-inspired approaches do not work well for charmed hadron decays due to the large expansion parameters
\alpha_{s}(m_{c})
and
\Lambda_{\rm QCD}/m_{c}
.
It is important to analyze charmed baryon decays through model calculation, such as final-state interaction (FSI), at the present stage.
The rescattering mechanism plays a key role in the theoretical analysis of doubly charmed baryon decays
Yu:2017zst
, contributing to the discovery of the doubly charmed baryon
\Xi_{cc}^{++}

LHCb:2017iph
.
Recently, the rescattering mechanism was applied to charmed baryon weak decays
Jia:2024pyb
.

In the rescattering mechanism, a charmed baryon first decays into a baryon and a meson via a short-distance emission diagram.
Then,
t
- and
s
-channel meson-baryon scattering between them serves as the long-distance contribution.
There are two approaches to obtain the FSI amplitudes contributing to a decay channel: calculating hadron-level Feynman diagrams directly using the chiral Lagrangian
Li:2020qrh
;
Jia:2024pyb
;
Jiang:2018oak
;
Hu:2024uia
;
Han:2021gkl
;
Han:2021azw
;
Yu:2017zst
, or extracting them from topological diagrams
Wang:2022wrb
;
Wang:2021rhd
;
Cheng:2004ru
;
Ablikim:2002ep
.
In the second method, both the rescattering amplitudes and the topological amplitudes are expressed in tensor form.
Tensor contractions are used to describe the transitions from the emission diagram to other topological diagrams.
The coefficients of the rescattering amplitudes can be derived from topological amplitudes
Wang:2022wrb
;
Wang:2021rhd
.

The theoretical framework for topological amplitudes in
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays is established in Ref.
Wang:2024ztg
.
However, it is not straightforward to extend the derivation of rescattering amplitudes from topological amplitudes to the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays.
The chiral Lagrangian for meson-baryon coupling is constructed using (1,1)-rank octet tensors, while the topological amplitudes for
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays are constructed using third-rank octet tensors, as each flavor index represents a quark line.
To connect the chiral Lagrangian at the hadron level with topological diagrams at the quark level, an intermediate bridge between them must be established.
In Ref.
Wang:2024ztg
, we constructed the ”(1,1)-rank amplitudes” via the (1,1)-rank octet tensors to derive the linear relations between topological amplitudes and the
SU(3)
irreducible amplitudes.
The (1,1)-rank amplitudes also enable the study of correlations between topological amplitudes and rescattering dynamics.

In this work, we generalize the theoretical framework proposed in
Wang:2021rhd
;
Wang:2022wrb
to charmed baryon decays.
Possible meson-meson or meson-baryon coupling configurations are constructed from (1,1)-rank amplitudes via tensor contractions.
Rescattering amplitudes derived from topological amplitudes are consistent with those derived directly from the chiral Lagrangian.
Isospin sum rules for all isospin systems in
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays are checked in terms of
s
- and
t
-channel rescattering amplitudes.
It is found that the rescattering amplitudes contributing to the quark-loop diagrams are comparable to the ones contributing to the tree diagrams, indicating potential observable
CP
violation in charmed baryon decays.

The Körner-Pati-Woo (KPW) theorem
Korner:1970xq
;
Pati:1970fg
, which states that the two quarks produced from the weak vertex are antisymmetric in flavor if both enter the low-lying baryon, is often used to reduce the amplitudes of heavy baryon decays.
However, the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is found to be inconsistent with rescattering dynamics.
Furthermore, the proof of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is questionable when color changes of quarks arising from gluons are considered.
To test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem, we suggest precisely measuring the branching fraction of the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}_{S}
mode on Belle (II).

This paper is organized as follows.
In Sec.
II
, we construct a theoretical framework relating topological amplitudes and rescattering amplitudes.
In Sec.
III
, we present phenomenological applications for rescattering amplitudes.
Sec.
IV
is a brief summary.
In Sec.
A
, the resacttering amplitudes induced by topological amplitudes in all isospin systems of
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays are derived to check the reliability of our framework.

II
From topologies to rescattering amplitudes

The effective Hamiltonian in charm quark weak decay in the SM is
Buchalla:1995vs

\mathcal{H}_{\rm eff}=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\sum_{q=d,s}V_{cq_{1}}^{*}V_{uq_{2}}\left(\sum_{q=1}^{2}C_{i}(\mu)\mathcal{O}_{i}(\mu)\right)-V_{cb}^{*}V_{ub}\left(\sum_{i=3}^{6}C_{i}(\mu)\mathcal{O}_{i}(\mu)+C_{8g}(\mu)\mathcal{O}_{8g}(\mu)\right)\right].

(1)

The magnetic-penguin contributions can be included into the Wilson coefficients for the penguin operators

Beneke:2003zv
;
Beneke:2000ry
;
Beneke:1999br
.
In the
SU(3)
picture, the weak Hamiltonian of charm decay can be written as

\mathcal{H}_{\rm eff}=\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{3}H_{ij}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{ij}^{k},\qquad{\rm where}\qquad\mathcal{O}_{ij}^{k}=\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{\rm color}\sum_{\rm current}C\,(\overline{q}_{i}q_{k})(\overline{q}_{j}c).

(2)

Similarly to the effective Hamiltonian, the initial and final states, including the pseudoscalar meson
P
, octet baryon
B_{8}
, and charmed anti-triplet baryon
B_{c\overline{3}}
, can be written as

\displaystyle|P^{\alpha}\rangle=(P^{\alpha})^{i}_{j}|P^{i}_{j}\rangle,\qquad|B^{\alpha}_{8}\rangle=(B^{\alpha}_{8})^{ijk}|B^{ijk}_{8}\rangle,\qquad|B^{\alpha}_{c\overline{3}}\rangle=|(B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}})_{rs}|[B_{c\overline{3}}]_{rs}\rangle,

(3)

where
|P^{i}_{j}\rangle
,
|B^{ijk}_{8}\rangle
and
|[B_{c\overline{3}}]_{rs}\rangle
are the quark compositions of the states, and
(P^{\alpha})
,
(B^{\alpha}_{8})^{ijk}
and
(B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}})_{rs}
are the coefficient matrices.
The decay amplitude of the
B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}}\to B^{\alpha}_{8}P^{\beta}
mode is constructed to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{\alpha}P^{\beta})

\displaystyle=\langle B^{\alpha}_{8}P^{\beta}|\mathcal{H}_{\rm eff}|B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rm Per.}\,(B^{\alpha}_{8})^{ijk}\langle B^{ijk}_{8}|(P^{\beta})^{l}_{m}\langle P^{l}_{m}||H_{np}^{q}\mathcal{O}_{np}^{q}||(B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}})_{rs}|[B_{c\overline{3}}]_{rs}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\rm Per.}\,\langle B^{ijk}_{8}P^{l}_{m}|\mathcal{O}_{np}^{q}|[B_{c\overline{3}}]_{rs}\rangle\times(B^{\alpha}_{8})^{ijk}(P^{\beta})^{l}_{m}H_{np}^{q}(B^{\gamma}_{c\overline{3}})_{rs}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\omega}X_{\omega}(C_{\omega})_{\alpha\beta\gamma}.

(4)

In above formula,
\sum_{\rm Per.}
presents summing over all possible full contractions,
\omega
is the reduced matrix element, and
(C_{\omega})_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
is the Clebsch-Gordan coefficient.
According to the Wigner-Eckhart theorem
Eckart30
;
Wigner59
,
X_{\omega}
is independent of the indices
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\gamma
.
All the information about the initial/final states is absorbed into the Clebsch-Gordan coefficient
(C_{\omega})_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
.

There are two different light baryon octets,
B_{8}^{S}
and
B_{8}^{A}
. The flavor wavefunctions of
B_{8}^{S}
and
B_{8}^{A}
are symmetric and anti-symmetric under
q_{1}\leftrightarrow q_{2}
, respectively.
Because of the different spin wavefunctions, the topological diagrams of charmed baryon decays into octet baryons have two different sets.
The total amplitude for the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decay is the sum of the amplitudes for the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{S}P
and
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{A}P
transitions,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P)=\mathcal{A}^{S}(B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{S}P)+\mathcal{A}^{A}(B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{A}P).

(5)

The theoretical framework of the topological amplitudes for the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays is established in Ref.
Wang:2024ztg
.
A one-to-one mapping between the topological diagram and the invariant tensor is built.
The topological diagrams contributing to the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{S}P
and
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}^{A}P
modes are presented in detail.
The completeness of topologies is confirmed by permutation.
The equations of
SU(3)
irreducible amplitudes decomposed by topologies are derived through two different approaches.

The chiral Lagrangians of the
VPP
,
BBP
, and
BBV
interactions are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{V}\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\,g_{VPP}\,Tr\,(\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\,[\mathcal{P},\partial_{\mu}\mathcal{P}]),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{P}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}D\,Tr(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\,i\gamma_{5}\{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B}\})+\sqrt{2}F\,Tr(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\,i\gamma_{5}[\mathcal{P},\mathcal{B}])-\sqrt{2}(D-F)Tr(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\,i\gamma_{5}\mathcal{B})Tr(\mathcal{P}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}D_{V}Tr(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\gamma^{\mu}\{\mathcal{V}_{\mu},\mathcal{B}\})+\sqrt{2}F_{V}Tr(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\gamma^{\mu}[\mathcal{V}_{\mu},\mathcal{B}])-\sqrt{2}(D_{V}-F_{V})Tr(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\gamma^{\mu}\mathcal{B})Tr(\mathcal{V}_{\mu}).

(6)

The charmed baryon anti-triplet is

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{c\overline{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\Xi_{c}^{0}\\
-\Xi_{c}^{+}\\
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\\
\end{array}\right).

The light pseudoscalar meson nonet is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\pi^{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\eta_{8}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\eta_{1}&\pi^{+}&K^{+}\\
\pi^{-}&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\pi^{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\eta_{8}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\eta_{1}&K^{0}\\
K^{-}&\overline{K}^{0}&-\sqrt{2/3}\eta_{8}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\eta_{1}\\
\end{array}\right).

The vector meson nonet is

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\rho^{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\omega&\rho^{+}&K^{*+}\\
\rho^{-}&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\rho^{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\omega&K^{*0}\\
K^{*-}&\overline{K}^{*0}&\phi\\
\end{array}\right).

The baryon octet is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{8}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Sigma^{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\Lambda^{0}&\Sigma^{+}&p\\
\Sigma^{-}&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\Sigma^{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\Lambda^{0}&n\\
\Xi^{-}&\Xi^{0}&-\sqrt{2/3}\Lambda^{0}\\
\end{array}\right).

Note that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{P}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{V}}
are constructed by (1,1)-rank octet tensors.
It is not straightforward to correlate Eq. (
II
) with topological amplitudes through Eq. (
II
).
To study the relation between them, we must establish a bridge between third-rank and (1,1)-rank octet tensors.
In Refs.
Wang:2025bdl
;
Wang:2024ztg
, it is found that third-rank tensors
(B^{S}_{8})^{ijk}
and
(B^{A}_{8})^{ijk}
can be expressed in terms of (1,1)-rank tensors as

\displaystyle(B^{S}_{8})^{ijk}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\left[\epsilon^{kil}(B_{8})^{j}_{l}+\epsilon^{kjl}(B_{8})^{i}_{l}\right],\qquad(B^{A}_{8})^{ijk}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\epsilon^{ijl}(B_{8})^{k}_{l}.

(23)

The ”(1,1)-rank amplitudes” are constructed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}M)

\displaystyle=A_{1}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{kl}P^{l}_{i}B^{k}_{j}+A_{2}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{lk}P^{l}_{j}B^{k}_{i}+A_{3}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{lk}P^{l}_{i}B^{k}_{j}+A_{4}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{kl}P^{l}_{j}B^{k}_{i}

\displaystyle+A_{5}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ik}P^{l}_{j}B^{k}_{l}+A_{6}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{il}P^{l}_{k}B^{k}_{j}+A_{7}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ki}P^{l}_{j}B^{k}_{l}+A_{8}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{li}P^{l}_{k}B^{k}_{j}

\displaystyle+A_{9}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ik}P^{l}_{l}B^{k}_{j}+A_{10}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ki}P^{l}_{l}B^{k}_{j}+A_{11}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{kj}P^{l}_{i}B^{k}_{l}+A_{12}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{lj}P^{l}_{k}B^{k}_{i}

\displaystyle+A_{13}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{kj}P^{l}_{l}B^{k}_{i}+A_{14}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ij}P^{l}_{k}B^{k}_{l}.

(24)

In above formula, we have neglected terms involving
H^{j}_{jk}
because no tree operators contribute to non-zero
H^{j}_{jk}
in the Standard Model.
we derive equations for the (1,1)-rank amplitudes decomposed in terms of topological amplitudes constructed using third-rank octet tensors.
Amplitudes
A_{1}\sim A_{10}
are found to receive contributions only from tree diagrams.
Amplitudes
A_{11}\sim A_{14}
include contributions from quark-loop diagrams.
Amplitude (
II
) is constructed in the
SU(3)_{F}
limit.
Under
SU(3)_{F}
symmetry, baryons expressed by the tensor
B^{l}_{l}
vanish.
In this work, we aim to identify relations between topological amplitudes and rescattering dynamics.
Following decay amplitudes beyond the
SU(3)F
limit are also analyzed in this work:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\prime}(B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}M)

\displaystyle=A_{15}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ki}P^{k}_{j}B^{l}_{l}+A_{16}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ik}P^{k}_{j}B^{l}_{l}+A_{17}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{kj}P^{k}_{i}B^{l}_{l}+A_{18}B_{c\overline{3}}^{i}H^{j}_{ij}P^{k}_{k}B^{l}_{l}.

(25)

In the rescattering mechanism, a charmed baryon first decays into a baryon and a meson via a short-distance emission diagram
T
, and then non-factorizable QCD effects are modeled as
t
- and
s
-channel meson-baryon scattering between the two particles generated from
T
.
According to Ref.
Wang:2024ztg
, the emission diagram contributes only to the (1,1)-rank topological amplitude
A_{2}
defined in Eq. (
II
).
If we neglect the short-distance contributions of other topological diagrams, as assumed in rescattering dynamics
Li:2020qrh
;
Jia:2024pyb
;
Jiang:2018oak
;
Hu:2024uia
;
Han:2021gkl
;
Han:2021azw
;
Yu:2017zst
, the long-distance rescattering contributions will describe transitions from amplitude
A_{2}
to other amplitudes and itself.
In this work, we consider only vector mesons and octet baryons as propagators, i.e.,
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P\to B_{8}P
via the exchange of a vector meson or octet baryon.
For other processes, the analytical approach is similar to that of the special case, and we do not present the details in this work.

In tensor language, the amplitudes for the
VPP
,
BBP
, and
BBV
strong interactions can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{VPP}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{+}P^{j}_{i}V^{i}_{k}P^{k}_{j}+\alpha^{-}P^{j}_{i}V^{k}_{j}P^{i}_{k},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{BBP}

\displaystyle=\beta^{+}B^{j}_{i}P^{i}_{k}B^{k}_{j}+\beta^{-}B^{j}_{i}P^{k}_{j}B^{i}_{k}+\beta^{0}B^{j}_{i}P^{k}_{k}B^{i}_{j},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{BBV}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{+}B^{j}_{i}V^{i}_{k}B^{k}_{j}+\gamma^{-}B^{j}_{i}V^{k}_{j}B^{i}_{k}+\gamma^{0}B^{j}_{i}V^{k}_{k}B^{i}_{j}.

(26)

By comparing Eq. (
II
) with Eq. (
II
), we find that the amplitudes
\alpha^{\pm}
,
\beta^{\pm,0}
and
\gamma^{\pm,0}
match the chiral Lagrangian as

\displaystyle\alpha^{+}

\displaystyle=-\alpha^{-}=\frac{ig_{VPP}}{\sqrt{2}}V^{\mu}P\partial_{\mu}P,

\displaystyle\beta^{+}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}(D+F)\,\overline{B}\,i\gamma_{5}PB,\qquad\beta^{-}=-\beta^{0}=\sqrt{2}(D-F)\,\overline{B}\,i\gamma_{5}BP,

\displaystyle\gamma^{+}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}(D_{V}+F_{V})\,\overline{B}\gamma^{\mu}V_{\mu}B,\qquad\gamma^{-}=-\gamma^{0}=\sqrt{2}(D_{V}-F_{V})\,\overline{B}\gamma^{\mu}BV_{\mu}.

(27)

For example, the tensor contraction for
p\pi^{0}p
in Eq. (
II
) is
\mathcal{A}_{p\pi^{0}p}=\beta^{+}\,(p)^{3}_{1}(\pi^{0})^{1}_{1}(p)^{1}_{3}=\beta^{+}/\sqrt{2}
,
which is consistent with the strong coupling in the chiral Lagrangian is
g_{{\pi^{0}}pp}=(D+F)
.

Figure 1:
The
t
- and
s
-channel rescattering amplitudes contributing to the
A_{5}
amplitude.

Following Ref.
Wang:2021rhd
, we express the transitions from
A_{2}
to
A_{i}
in tensor form.
Taking the
A_{2}\to A_{5}
transition as an example, the
t
-channel rescattering amplitude is written as

\displaystyle T(A_{5})[i,j,k,l]\,=\,B_{c\overline{3}}^{q}H_{pm}^{n}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.90451pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=37.2685pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.90451pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 4.78334pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 2.62242pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule 3.41667pt}}B_{q}^{m}\,\cdot\,P_{p}^{n}B_{m}^{q}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0434pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=21.04236pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0434pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=21.04236pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.1915pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=18.51196pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 4.78334pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 2.70923pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=17.46411pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 3.41667pt}}B_{n}^{m}\,\cdot\,B_{r}^{l}P_{k}^{k}B_{l}^{r}\,\cdot\,\delta_{ip}\delta_{jm}\delta_{jn}\,\cdot\,\delta_{iq}\,\cdot\,\delta_{lr},

(82)

\displaystyle S(A_{18})_{2}[i,j,k,l]\,=\,B_{c\overline{3}}^{q}H_{pm}^{n}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.90451pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=37.2685pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.90451pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=37.2685pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.08385pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=32.02525pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 4.78334pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 2.62242pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=30.05453pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 3.41667pt}}P_{n}^{p}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0434pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt\vrule width=37.2685pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0434pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt\vrule width=37.2685pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.1915pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt\vrule width=32.02525pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 4.78334pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 2.70923pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt\vrule width=30.05453pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=5.69054pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 3.41667pt}}B_{q}^{m}\,\cdot\,P_{p}^{n}B_{m}^{q}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0434pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=21.04236pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 4.0434pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=21.04236pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 6.83331pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 3.1915pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=18.51196pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 4.78334pt}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\kern 2.70923pt\hbox{\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt\vrule width=17.46411pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.50003pt\vrule width=0.50003pt,height=0.0pt,depth=2.84526pt}\hss}\vskip 2.15277pt\vskip 3.41667pt}}B_{q}^{p}\,\cdot\,B_{r}^{l}P_{k}^{k}B_{l}^{r}\,\cdot\,\delta_{ip}\delta_{jm}\delta_{jn}\,\cdot\,\delta_{iq}\,\cdot\,\delta_{lr}.

(83)

The
A_{2}\to A_{i}
transitions, represented by Eqs. (
28
)
\sim
(
83
), cover the possible sub-structures of meson-baryon scattering without repetition.
In the rest of the paper, we will use the above rescattering structures to derive the rescattering amplitudes, and compare them with the results derived from chiral Lagrangian.

III
Phenomenological analysis

III.1
Test isospin symmetry in
\Xi_{c}\to\Xi\pi
system

In this subsection, we take
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{-}\pi^{+}
,
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{0}
, and
\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{+}
modes as examples to test rescattering amplitudes derived from topological amplitudes.
The decay amplitude for the
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{-}\pi^{+}
mode is
\lambda_{1}(A_{2}+A_{5})
.
The long-distance contributions modeled by triangle diagrams at hadron level include

\displaystyle T(A_{2})_{4}[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-})-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{-}),

\displaystyle T(A_{2})_{5}[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{-}),

\displaystyle T(A_{2})_{6}[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{-}),

\displaystyle T(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-})+\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{-}).

(84)

To distinguish different strong coupling constants, we add subscripts
\alpha^{\pm},\beta^{\pm,0},\gamma^{\pm,0}
for rescattering amplitudes.
In
T(A_{2})_{4}[u,d,s,u]
, the quark constituent of the vector meson is
d\overline{d}
when it couples with pseudoscalar mesons, and
u\overline{u}
when it couples with octet baryons.
Since
d\overline{d}=-\rho^{0}/\sqrt{2}+\omega/\sqrt{2}
and
u\overline{u}=\rho^{0}/\sqrt{2}+\omega/\sqrt{2}
, the vector propagator is
-\rho^{0}/2+\omega/2
.
In
T(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]
, the quark constituent of the vector meson is
u\overline{u}
and the vector propagator is
\rho^{0}/2+\omega/2
.
The
VPP
couplings in
T(A_{2})_{4}[u,d,s,u]
and
T(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]
are
\alpha^{-}
and
\alpha^{+}
respectively,
and the
VBB
coupling in both is
\gamma^{-}
.
Given that
\alpha^{-}=-\alpha^{+}
, the vector propagators in
T(A_{2})_{4}[u,d,s,u]
and
T(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]
become
\rho^{0}/2-\omega/2
and
\rho^{0}/2+\omega/2
, respectively.
Only the
\omega
meson contributes to
T(A_{2})_{5}[u,d,s,u]
and
T(A_{2})_{6}[u,d,s,u]

because the
VBB
coupling of them is
\gamma^{0}B^{j}_{i}V^{k}_{k}B^{i}_{j}
.
Amplitude
A_{5}
also receives a long-distance contribution modeled by a bubble diagram,

\displaystyle S(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Theta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-}).

(85)

Summing all FSI amplitudes for the
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{-}\pi^{+}
decay yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{-}\pi^{+})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}\{T(A_{2})_{4}[u,d,s,u]+T(A_{2})_{5}[u,d,s,u]+T(A_{2})_{6}[u,d,s,u]

(86)

\displaystyle~~~~~~+T(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]+S(A_{5})[u,d,s,u]\}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}[\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-})+\Theta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-})].

(87)

It should be noted that the contributions associated with the
\omega\pi\pi
coupling cancel each other out.

The decay amplitude for the
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{0}
decay is
\lambda_{1}(A_{3}-A_{5})/\sqrt{2}
.
The long-distance contributions include

\displaystyle T(A_{3})_{1}[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\rho^{+},\pi^{0},\Xi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{3})_{2}[u,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{5})[u,d,s,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\rho^{+},\pi^{0},\Xi^{0}),

\displaystyle S(A_{5})[u,d,s,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Theta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{0},\Xi^{0}).

(88)

Summing
A_{3}
and
A_{5}
amplitudes, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{0})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{1}\{T(A_{3})_{1}[u,d,s,u]+T(A_{3})_{2}[u,d,s,u]-T(A_{5})[u,d,s,d]-S(A_{5})[u,d,s,d]\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}\lambda_{1}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\rho^{+},\pi^{0},\Xi^{0})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{1}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{0})

\displaystyle~~~~-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{1}\Theta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{0}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{0},\Xi^{0}).

(89)

The decay amplitude for the
\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{+}
decay is
-\lambda_{1}(A_{2}+A_{3})
.
The rescattering contributions include

\displaystyle T(A_{2})_{1}[d,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0})+\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{2})_{5}[d,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{2})_{6}[d,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{3})_{1}[d,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0})-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Xi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{3})_{2}[d,d,s,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{+}).

(90)

Summing
A_{2}
and
A_{3}
amplitudes, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{+})=

\displaystyle-\lambda_{1}\{T(A_{2})_{1}[d,d,s,u]+T(A_{2})_{5}[d,d,s,u]+T(A_{2})_{6}[d,d,s,u]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~+T(A_{3})_{1}[d,d,s,u]+T(A_{3})_{2}[d,d,s,u]\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0})-\lambda_{1}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Xi^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Xi^{0},\Xi^{-},\Xi^{0},\pi^{+}).

(91)

Again, all contributions associated with the
\omega\pi\pi
vertex cancel each other out.

Equations (
86
), (
III.1
) and (
III.1
) can also be derived directly from the chiral Lagrangian (
II
).
Starting from topological amplitudes, we obtain the same results.
This indicates a correlation between topological amplitudes and chiral dynamics.
The decay modes for the
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{-}\pi^{+}
,
\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{0}
and
\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{+}
modes form an isospin sum rule,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{-}\pi^{+})+\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}(\Xi^{0}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{0})+\mathcal{A}(\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Xi^{0}\pi^{+})=0.

(92)

Under the isospin symmetry, particles in an isospin multiplet can be treated as identical in
\Delta
and
\Theta
.
The isospin sum rule (
92
) can be verified in terms of
t
- and
s
-channel rescattering amplitudes.
Tests of isospin sum rules in other charmed baryon decay systems are presented in Appendix
A
.

III.2
Baryon .
vs
. Meson

In this subsection, we compare rescattering amplitudes between meson and baryon systems.
The detailed analysis for the relation between topological amplitudes and rescattering amplitudes in charmed meson decays can be found in Ref.
Wang:2021rhd
.

•

The decay amplitude for a singly Cabibbo-suppressed charmed hadron decay mode is written as
\mathcal{A}=\lambda_{d}\mathcal{A}_{d}+\lambda_{s}\mathcal{A}_{s}
.
Then the
CP
violation can then be expressed as

\displaystyle A_{CP}^{\rm dir}\simeq-2\,\frac{{Im}(\lambda_{d}^{*}\lambda_{s})}{|\lambda_{d}|^{2}}\frac{{Im}(\mathcal{A}_{d}^{*}\mathcal{A}_{s})}{|\mathcal{A}_{d}-\mathcal{A}_{s}|^{2}}.

(93)

In
D\to PP
decays, the quark-loop diagram
P
is the primary source of
CP
asymmetries, as its long-distance effect induced by rescattering dynamics is comparable to tree diagrams.
In the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays, amplitudes
A_{11}
and
A_{12}
are the primary sources of
CP
asymmetries.
Similarly to the
D
meson decays, the rescattering contributions in
A_{11}
and
A_{12}
are comparable to the tree amplitudes.
It is possible that
CP
violation in some charmed baryon decay channels could reach
\mathcal{O}(10^{-4})
or even
\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})
.

•

For charmed meson decays, only mesons can serve as intermediate propagators.
The
\mathcal{V}\mathcal{P}\mathcal{P}
coupling has only one coupling constant.
Under
SU(3)
symmetry, the long-distance contributions for the
E
and
P
diagrams are equivalent
Wang:2021rhd
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(E)=\mathcal{A}_{L}(P).

(94)

For charmed baryon decays, both the meson and baryon serve as intermediate propagators.
The
\mathcal{P}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{V}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}
couplings each have two coupling constants.
A simple relation like Eq. (
94
) is not found in charmed baryon decays.

•

In Ref.
Wang:2021rhd
and this work, we factor out the coefficients of meson and baryon tensors in triangle and bubble diagrams.
In meson sector, both pseudoscalar and vector mesons form nonets.
The tensor representation of a meson
M^{i}_{j}
, for example
M^{u}_{u}
, can be written as
1/\sqrt{2}\pi^{0}+1/\sqrt{6}\eta_{8}+1/\sqrt{3}\eta_{1}
.
The contraction of two
M^{u}_{u}
terms serves as a propagator in rescattering amplitudes, written as
\pi^{0}/2+\eta_{8}/6+\eta_{1}/3
.
In baryon sector, spin-1/2 ground-state baryons form an octet.
There is no spin-1/2 ground-state singlet baryon due to symmetry constraints.
The tensor representation
B^{u}_{u}
is written as
1/\sqrt{2}\Sigma^{0}+1/\sqrt{6}\Lambda^{0}
.
The propagator constructed by contracting two
B^{u}_{u}
term is
\Sigma^{0}/2+\Lambda^{0}/6
.
The absence of a singlet propagator can be considered a
SU(3)_{F}
breaking effect.
It can be expected that
SU(3)_{F}
breaking effects in charmed baryon decays are greater than those in charmed meson decays.

III.3
Test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem

In the literature, the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem
Pati:1970fg
;
Korner:1970xq
plays an important role in analyzing charmed baryon weak decays.
It stress that the two quarks produced by weak operators must be antisymmetric in flavor if they enter the same low-lying baryon.
The Körner-Pati-Woo theorem can be explained by following argument.
The four-quark operator for weak decay is

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(x)=\{\overline{q}_{\alpha,i}(x)(1-\gamma_{5})q_{\beta,j}(x)\}\{\overline{q}_{\gamma,k}(x)(1-\gamma_{5})q_{\delta,l}(x)\},

(95)

where
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\gamma
, and
\delta
are color indices, and
i
,
j
,
k
, and
l
are flavor indices.
It can be written as the following formula via Fierz transformation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(x)=\{\overline{q}_{\gamma,k}(x)(1-\gamma_{5})q_{\beta,j}(x)\}\{\overline{q}_{\alpha,i}(x)(1-\gamma_{5})q_{\delta,l}(x)\}.

(96)

Thus,
\mathcal{O}(x)
is symmetric under the interchange
(\alpha,i)\leftrightarrow(\gamma,k)
.
If two quarks produced by weak operators enter a final-state baryon, only the color-antisymmetric part of
\mathcal{O}(x)
under the interchange
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma
contributes, since the low-lying baryon is an
SU(3)_{C}
singlet.
As a consequence, the two quarks must be antisymmetric in flavor.

By applying the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem and isospin symmetry to topological diagrams, we can derive relations between the branching fractions of heavy baryon weak decays, which extend beyond isospin sum rules.
The decay amplitudes for the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}
and
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
decays are
Wang:2024ztg

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{d}(A_{1}+A_{11})+\lambda_{s}(A_{7}+A_{11}),

(97)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{d}(-A_{3}+A_{11})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{s}(A_{7}+A_{11}).

(98)

If the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is valid for charmed baryon decays, we obtain
A_{1}=-A_{3}
in the isospin limit
Wang:2024ztg
;
Wang:2025bdl
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+})=\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}).

(99)

The above equation was also derived in Ref.
Geng:2019xbo
.
Equation (
99
) holds when considering
SU(3)_{F}
breaking effects.
However, it is violated if the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem does not hold.
Isospin breaking is naively predicted to be
\mathcal{O}(1\%)
.
If Eq. (
99
) is significantly violated, it indicates the violation of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.

Equation (
99
) does not hold in rescattering dynamics.
The rescattering contributions to the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}
decay include

\displaystyle T(A_{1})_{1}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Sigma^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0})+\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{1})_{2}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{1})_{4}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{1}[s,s,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{+},K^{0},\Sigma^{+}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{2}[s,s,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{+},K^{0},\Sigma^{+}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{3}[s,s,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Xi^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0}),

\displaystyle S(A_{7})_{1}[s,s,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},p,K^{0},\Sigma^{+}),

\displaystyle T(A_{11})[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Sigma^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0})+\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0}),

\displaystyle S(A_{11})[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,p,K^{0},\Sigma^{+}),

\displaystyle T(A_{11})[s,s,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{2}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Xi^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0}),

\displaystyle S(A_{11})[s,s,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{2}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},p,K^{0},\Sigma^{+}).

(100)

The rescattering contributions to the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
decay include

\displaystyle T(A_{3})_{1}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,K^{*0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{3})_{2}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Sigma^{-},\Sigma^{0},K^{+}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{1}[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{2}[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{3}[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle S(A_{7})_{1}[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle T(A_{11})[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,\Sigma^{-},\Sigma^{0},K^{+}),

\displaystyle S(A_{11})[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,p,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{11})[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle S(A_{11})[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{2}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},p,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{1}[s,s,d,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{2}[s,s,d,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{5}[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{6}[s,s,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\phi,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{5}[s,s,d,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{6}[s,s,d,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\phi,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}).

(101)

In the above formula, we include contributions from the vanishing amplitudes
A_{15}^{u}
and
A_{15}^{d}
, where the superscripts
u
and
d
denote the quark components of the
\Sigma^{0}
baryon.
Summing all contributions, the rescattering amplitudes contributing to the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}
and
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
modes are

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{d}\Delta_{(\beta^{+}+\beta^{-}),(2\beta^{+}-\beta^{-})}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},\pi^{+},n,\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0})

\displaystyle~~+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{d}\Delta_{(\beta^{+}-\beta^{-}),\beta^{-}}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},\pi^{+},n,\Sigma^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0})

\displaystyle~~-\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{s}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},(\gamma^{+}+\gamma^{-})}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{+},K^{0},\Sigma^{+})

\displaystyle~~-\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{s}\Delta_{\beta^{+},(\beta^{+}-2\beta^{-})}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Xi^{0},\Sigma^{+},K^{0})

\displaystyle~~+\lambda_{d}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,p,K^{0},\Sigma^{+})

\displaystyle~~+\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{s}\Theta_{(2\beta^{+}-\beta^{-}),\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},p,K^{0},\Sigma^{+}),

(102)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{d}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},\pi^{+},n,\overline{K}^{*0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0})

\displaystyle~~+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{d}\Delta_{(\beta^{+}-\beta^{-}),\beta^{-}}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},\pi^{+},n,\Sigma^{-},\Sigma^{0},K^{+})

\displaystyle~~-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{s}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},(\gamma^{+}+\gamma^{-})}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},K^{+},\Sigma^{0})

\displaystyle~~-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{s}\Delta_{\beta^{+},(\beta^{+}-2\beta^{-})}(\Lambda_{c}^{+},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Xi^{-},\Sigma^{0},K^{+})

\displaystyle~~+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{d}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},n,p,K^{+},\Sigma^{0})

\displaystyle~~+\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}\lambda_{s}\Theta_{(2\beta^{+}-\beta^{-}),\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},K^{+},\Lambda^{0},p,K^{+},\Sigma^{0}).

(103)

Note that Eq. (
99
) is violated in Eqs. (
III.3
) and (
III.3
).
The two particles generated from the emission diagram
T^{SD}
rescatter into final-state particles by exchanging a baryon in the first term of Eq. (
III.3
), while exchanging a meson in the first term of Eq. (
III.3
).
The first terms in Eqs. (
III.3
) and (
III.3
) include rescattering contributions from the decay amplitudes
A_{1}
and
A_{3}
, respectively.
Thus, Eqs. (
III.3
) and (
III.3
) indicate that the relation
A_{1}=-A_{3}
is violated in rescattering dynamics.

The final-state particles in Eq. (
99
), such as
K^{+}
and
K^{0}
mesons, can be replaced by excited-state particles
K^{*+}
and
K^{*0}
mesons.
The branching fraction of the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{*0}
mode is twice that of
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{*+}
mode according to Eq. (
99
).
In Ref.
Jia:2024pyb
, the branching fractions for
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{*0}
and
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{*+}
are calculated to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}r(\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{*0})=2.10^{+1.37}_{-0.86}\times 10^{-3},\qquad\mathcal{B}r(\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{*+})=1.60^{+0.89}_{-0.62}\times 10^{-3}.

(104)

It is found Eq. (
99
) is violated.
The fact that the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is violated by rescattering dynamics is also obtained in other decay channels.
See Refs.
Li:2020qrh
;
Han:2021gkl
;
Hu:2024uia
;
Jiang:2018oak
;
Jia:2024pyb
;
Han:2021azw
for additional cases.

Figure 2:
Two topological diagrams contributing to the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
and
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}
decays, respectively.

Figure 3:
The long-distance contributions in the topologies
E_{B}
and
E_{M}
, where
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\gamma
,
\delta
and
\epsilon
are color indices.

The conclusion that the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is violated by rescattering dynamics can be explained using topological diagrams.
The two diagrams in Fig.
2
, which contribute to the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
and
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}
decays, respectively
Wang:2024ztg
.
They satisfy the relation
E_{B}=-E_{M}
according to the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.
The long-distance rescattering contributions in
E_{B}
and
E_{M}
are shown in Fig.
3
.
Note that the two particles generated from the emission diagram
T^{SD}
rescatter into final-state particles by exchanging a meson in
T\to E_{B}
and a baryon in
T\to E_{M}
, as in Eqs. (
III.3
) and (
III.3
).
Considering the color restraints induced by the final color singlets, we have
\delta=\epsilon
in
T\to E_{B}
and
\gamma=\delta=\epsilon
in
T\to E_{M}
.
Thus,
E_{B}
is color-favored, and
E_{M}
is color-suppressed.
As a consequence, the equation
E_{B}=-E_{M}
is not valid in this case.

Let us revisit the proof of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.
The key step is that the color indices of
\mathcal{O}(x)
is antisymmetric if two quarks produced by weak operators enter the low-lying baryon.
The premise of this statement is that the color of quarks cannot change from production to confinement into a baryon.
However, the strong interaction propagators, gluons, form a color octet.
The color of quarks can change through gluon exchanges.
This is a loophole in the proof of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.
Thus, we prefer that the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is incorrect and cannot to used to reduce topological diagrams in charmed baryon decays.

The branching fractions for the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
and
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}_{S}
modes are measuring by the BESIII collaboration as
BESIII:2022wxj
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}r(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+})

\displaystyle=(4.7\pm 0.9\pm 0.1\pm 0.3)\times 10^{-4},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}r(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}_{S})

\displaystyle=(4.8\pm 1.4\pm 0.2\pm 0.3)\times 10^{-4}.

(105)

The data seem to support the validity of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem for
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays.
However, the Belle(II) collaboration reported the branching fraction for the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
decay as
Belle:2022uod

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}r(\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+})

\displaystyle=(3.58\pm 0.19\pm 0.06\pm 0.19)\times 10^{-4}.

(106)

This value is smaller than the branching fraction for the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
decay.
To test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem, a more precise measurement of the branching fraction for the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}_{S}
mode is crucial.

Due to the well-defined flavor symmetry of decuplet baryons, there are more decay channels to test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem in the
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{10}P
decays.
In Ref.
Wang:2024nxb
, we propose several equations involving singly Cabibbo-suppressed modes can be used to test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.
We can also use Cabibbo-favored
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{10}P
modes to test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem under
SU(3)_{F}
symmetry.
According to the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem, the branching fractions of
\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{K}^{0}
and
\Xi^{+}_{c}\to\Xi^{*0}\pi^{+}
modes are zero.
These conclusions are not valid after including
SU(3)_{F}
breaking effects.
Compared to the
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{0}K^{+}
and
\Lambda_{c}^{+}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}
decays, they might not be the golden modes in testing the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem.

IV
Summary

In this work, we investigate the relation between topological amplitudes at the quark level and final-state interactions at the hadron level in
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays.
The chiral Lagrangian is constructed using (1,1)-rank octet tensors.
The (1,1)-rank amplitudes, which are linear combinations of topological diagrams, are used to match the chiral Lagrangian.
Possible meson-meson and meson-baryon coupling configurations are identified.
Rescattering amplitudes derived from topological diagrams are consistent with those derived from the chiral Lagrangian.
Isospin relations in all isospin systems for
B_{c\overline{3}}\to B_{8}P
decays are tested in terms of
s
- and
t
-channel rescattering amplitudes.
Rescattering amplitudes contributing to quark-loop diagrams are found to be comparable to those contributing to tree diagrams, suggesting that observable
CP
violation in charmed baryon decays.
Furthermore, it is found that the Körner-Pati-Woo theoremis not consistent with rescattering dynamics.
The proof of the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem is questionable when color changes of quarks due to gluon exchanges are considered.
To test the Körner-Pati-Woo theorem, we suggest precisely measuring the branching fraction of the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\Sigma^{+}K^{0}_{S}
mode on Belle (II).

This work was supported in part by the National Natural Science Foundation of China under Grants No. 12105099.

A.1
\Lambda_{c}\to\Sigma\pi
system

The decay amplitude for the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\pi^{+}\Sigma^{0}
mode is
\lambda_{1}(A_{7}-A_{8})/\sqrt{2}
.
The rescattering amplitudes contribute to the
\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\pi^{+}\Sigma^{0}
decay include

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{1}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{2}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{3}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{-},\Sigma^{0},\pi^{+}),

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{4}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{-},\Sigma^{0},\pi^{+}),

\displaystyle S(A_{7})_{1}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle S(A_{7})_{2}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{8})_{1}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})+\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{8})_{2}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})+\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{8})_{3}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle T(A_{8})_{4}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{-},\Sigma^{0},\pi^{+}),

\displaystyle S(A_{8})_{2}[s,d,d,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{1}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{2}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{3}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})+\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})+\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{5}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{6}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{5}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{15})_{6}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},\gamma^{0}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\omega,\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}).

(107)

Summing all long-distance contributions, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{L}(\Lambda^{+}_{c}\to\pi^{+}\Sigma^{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{3}\lambda_{1}\Delta_{\alpha^{+},(\gamma^{+}+\gamma^{-})}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\rho^{0},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0})

\displaystyle~~+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{6}\lambda_{1}\Theta_{(\beta^{+}+\beta^{-}),(\beta^{+}-\beta^{-})}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{+},\Sigma^{0}).

(108)

.

\displaystyle T(A_{7})_{1}[s,d,u,d]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{0})-\frac{1}{18}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{0}),

\displaystyle T(A_{8})_{4}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{6}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{0})-\frac{1}{18}\Delta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{-}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{0}),

\displaystyle S(A_{8})_{1}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{-},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{0},\Sigma^{+}),

\displaystyle S(A_{8})_{2}[s,d,u,u]\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{3}\Theta_{\beta^{+},\beta^{+}}(\Lambda^{+}_{c},\pi^{+},\Lambda^{0},\Sigma^{+},\pi^{0},\Sigma^{+}),
